## What #545 is (plain engineering language)

#545 changed five register arms of the DC21285 "footbridge" device model.  Before it, a guest WRITE to any of
four read-only status registers (IRQ_STATUS +0x180, IRQ_RAW_STATUS +0x184, FIQ_STATUS +0x280, FIQ_RAW_STATUS
+0x284) or a guest READ of the write-only IRQEnableClear (+0x18c) ended the emulator process.  After it, each
such access is DECLINED -- no register changes, a declined read returns 0 -- and ANNOUNCED ONCE per site
through a per-site warn-once latch (`warned_*`).  The detector is `regress/diff_footbridge.c` (an offline
differential that `#include`s the real `dev_footbridge.c` and calls its access function directly) plus
`regress/footbridge_sites_probe.py` (a 37-row guest probe, unchanged by this repair).

## The chain so far (every step measured; artifacts under `regress/artifacts/fbd5/`)

1. pass 1, pass 2, the AF2 findings-sharing pass, and the flagship seat's regress/adjudication pass.
2. The flagship seat PREDICTED, by reading, that five one-condition edits to the DIRECTION test (Q1..Q5, e.g.
   `if (writeflag == MEM_READ && d->irq_enable)` at IRQ_STATUS) would pass every row.  Measured TRUE:
   333/333 offline and 37/37 guest for each (`q2_20260923/Q2.log`).  -> REPAIR 1 (X9 = legal x latches clear,
   plus a "wide" seed state), 491 rows.
3. Repair 1's own pass 2 (panel `panel_20260923_143458` + the Opus measuring seat + a dedicated codex job):
   codex, grok, glm and the kimi substitute DO NOT SHIP; agy, deepseek and minimax SHIP.  The Opus seat built
   434 one-line variants of the five arms and the head predicates: **153 passed all 491 rows**.
4. That was the THIRD consecutive pass to find cells of ONE space, so the carrier's class-closure amendment
   governed: REPAIR 2 enumerates the whole space from the handler's own inputs, closes it in ONE diff
   (X10, X11, X12 and a fourth "ones" state), and rewrites the CONTRACT to name what is covered and what is
   not.  **THIS PASS VERIFIES THAT ENUMERATION -- it is not asked to hunt new cells one at a time.**

## AC �?" SETTLED PREMISES (recorded resolutions; do not re-derive, but say if the diff or the handler contradicts one)

1. **The product change is settled** -- decline + once-latched announce at the five sites, 0 for the declined
   read.  Pass 1, pass 2, AF2 and the adjudication all ruled on it; neither repair touches it.
2. **The floor these rows answer to** (the carrier's DEPENDS-ON rule, bounded): the predicates DOMINATING each
   edited decline, walked to the function boundary, crossed with the body-read lvalues' BODY-TESTED partitions
   where they are jointly reachable; a value or combination the bodies never test as a case is DISCLOSED
   (clause e), not rowed.
3. **Insertion-class edits in OTHER cases** (a writer added at +0x188, the FIQ enable pair, TIMER_1_VALUE) are
   outside that closure (adjudication Q1).  Their existing rows stay, ungraded as floor.
4. **No guest reset-state reads are added**: every seat that answered repair 1's Q5 agreed the offline rows carry
   the floor; codex added that guest reads would buy integration coverage, not floor coverage.
5. **`fiq_status` has NO writer in the product** (grok, Opus): every FIQ status value these rows seed is synthetic.
   `irq_status` is written ONLY by `footbridge_interrupt_assert` / `_deassert` (`d->irq_status |= interrupt->line`,
   `d->irq_status &= ~interrupt->line`), which the offline harness stubs.

## The handler -- `DEVICE_ACCESS(footbridge)`, the parts this closure is about (verbatim, frozen copy)

NOT SHOWN: the PCI-config, UART and timer arms between `switch (relative_addr) {` and `case IRQ_STATUS:`, and the
timer arms after `case FIQ_ENABLE_CLEAR:`.  They read and write no field the five arms read, EXCEPT that a timer
tick reaches `irq_status` through the interrupt callbacks (see AC.5).

```c
DEVICE_ACCESS(footbridge)
{
	struct footbridge_data *d = (struct footbridge_data *) extra;
	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
	int timer_nr = 0;

	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);

	if (relative_addr >= TIMER_1_LOAD && relative_addr <= TIMER_4_CLEAR) {
		timer_nr = (relative_addr >> 5) & (N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS - 1);
		relative_addr &= ~0x060;
	}

	switch (relative_addr) {
	/* ... NOT SHOWN: VENDOR_ID, DEVICE_ID, PCI config, UART ... */

	case IRQ_STATUS:
		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
			odata = d->irq_status & d->irq_enable;
		else if (!d->warned_irq_status_w) {
			d->warned_irq_status_w = 1;
			fatal("[ WARNING: footbridge write to irq status? "
			    "(once) ]\n");
		}
		break;

	case IRQ_RAW_STATUS:
		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
			odata = d->irq_status;
		else if (!d->warned_irq_raw_w) {
			d->warned_irq_raw_w = 1;
			fatal("[ footbridge write to irq_raw_status (once) ]\n");
		}
		break;

	case IRQ_ENABLE_SET:
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			d->irq_enable |= idata;
			if (d->irq_status & d->irq_enable)
				INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->irq);
			else
				INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->irq);
		} else {
			odata = d->irq_enable;
		}
		break;

	case IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR:
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			d->irq_enable &= ~idata;
			if (d->irq_status & d->irq_enable)
				INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->irq);
			else
				INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->irq);
		} else if (!d->warned_irq_clear_r) {
			d->warned_irq_clear_r = 1;
			fatal("[ WARNING: footbridge read from "
			    "ENABLE CLEAR? (once) ]\n");
		}
		break;

	case FIQ_STATUS:
		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
			odata = d->fiq_status & d->fiq_enable;
		else if (!d->warned_fiq_status_w) {
			d->warned_fiq_status_w = 1;
			fatal("[ WARNING: footbridge write to fiq status? "
			    "(once) ]\n");
		}
		break;

	case FIQ_RAW_STATUS:
		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
			odata = d->fiq_status;
		else if (!d->warned_fiq_raw_w) {
			d->warned_fiq_raw_w = 1;
			fatal("[ footbridge write to fiq_raw_status (once) ]\n");
		}
		break;

	case FIQ_ENABLE_SET:
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
			d->fiq_enable |= idata;
		break;

	case FIQ_ENABLE_CLEAR:
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
			d->fiq_enable &= ~idata;
		break;

	/* ... NOT SHOWN: the timer arms ... */

	default:if (writeflag == MEM_READ) {
			fatal("[ footbridge: read from 0x%x ]\n",
			    (int)relative_addr);
		} else {
			fatal("[ footbridge: write to 0x%x: 0x%llx ]\n",
			    (int)relative_addr, (long long)idata);
		}
	}

	if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
		memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len, odata);

	return 1;
}
```
(The `#545:` and `#539:` comments inside the arms are omitted here; nothing else is.)  Offsets: IRQ_STATUS 0x180,
IRQ_RAW_STATUS 0x184, IRQ_ENABLE_SET 0x188, IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR 0x18c, FIQ_STATUS 0x280, FIQ_RAW_STATUS 0x284,
FIQ_ENABLE_SET 0x288, FIQ_ENABLE_CLEAR 0x28c; the timer range is TIMER_1_LOAD 0x300 .. TIMER_4_CLEAR 0x36c.

**F1 -- legal access with the latches SET, the VALUE channel** (grok, codex seat, Opus b_neg; kimi-substitute on

value over its width, and a legal IRQ Clear write still clears and drives (5 registers x 3 widths + 1 write, x 4
states = 64 rows).  Measured: b_neg 10 green -> 0.
**F2 -- the cross-site latch ORDER** (Opus fl_neg / or_L on a later latch; kimi-substitute).  Every multi-site
sequence declined the sites in one fixed order, so a site's first decline was never made with a LATER site
already latched.  **ACCEPTED -> X11**: each ordered pair (j latched, then i's first decline announces exactly once,
keeps every field and the output) -- 20 rows, seeded.  Measured: fl_neg 10 -> 0; or_L 14 -> 4 (the 4 left are the
equivalent own-latch forms).
**F3 -- the legal Clear write's WIDTH** (codex seat Q3, Opus c_dir).  Every legal write was len 4.  **ACCEPTED ->
X12** (Clear write 0x70 at len 1 and 2).  c_dir 3 -> 0.
**F4 -- the legal Clear write's DATA** (Opus `!(idata >> 8)`; codex-dedicated V9 `~(idata & 0xff)`).  Clear data was
only 0 / 0x70.  **ACCEPTED -> X12** (all-ones data at len 1, 2 and 4).  V9: green -> 3 red.
**F5 -- a condition keyed on a field BIT no state sets** (grok `irq_enable & 0x100`, agy `irq_status & 1`, kimi-
substitute `irq_enable & 1`, glm `fiq_enable & 0x100`, Opus bit_*).  **ACCEPTED -> a fourth state, ONES**
(0xffffffff in all four fields) in X6, X7, X9, X10 and X12: with all-zero, every single-bit condition on one field
diverts in one of the two.  bit_* 50 -> 0.
**F6 -- byte-lane masks on a read value or a decline-body store** (Opus lane_*).  **ACCEPTED -> ONES** as above.
lane 10 -> 0.
**F7 -- the two HEAD predicates dominate every decline and were missing from my AC.2 list** (codex-dedicated T2/T3,
Opus Q1(a)): the write-data fetch `if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) idata = ...` and the timer-fold range test.
**REFINED, not rowed separately**: (i) the fetch feeds only `idata`, which NO decline reads; on the legal side its
`&& len == 4` / `&& len != 1` forms are now RED (X12's narrow Clear writes), and a single-field zero/nonzero
condition on it is held by X9/X12's writes from the all-zero and ones states; (ii) the fold clears only bits 5-6,
and all five offsets (0x180, 0x184, 0x18c, 0x280, 0x284) have bits 5-6 clear, so a WIDENED fold (Opus dom 2) is
EQUIVALENT at the five and only aliases OTHER offsets onto the arms (the address axis, disclosed); a NARROWED fold
(codex's nine timer-range x field pairs) changes only timer registers (other registers' semantics, disclosed).
**F8 -- conditions on one EXACT field value** (Opus e_dir/e_flag 40; codex-dedicated V3; glm on repair 1's
contract).  **DISCLOSED (clause e)**: the bodies test no values, and no finite set of states holds every value.
The contract now names it.
**F9 -- "bits 8-31 are exercised" and the X8 row names "(all 32 bits)" overclaim** (glm, codex seat, Opus Q1(c)).
**ACCEPTED for the comments** (gate comment rewritten; the X8 comment now says the wide seeds set bytes 0, 2 and 3
and the ONES state exercises byte 1).  **Row NAMES kept**: renaming breaks the carried-kills row identity; "(all 32
bits)" means the len-4 read is compared over all 32 bits of its result.
**F10 -- X9's header said "every legal access"** (codex seat Q4, codex-dedicated T3, kimi-substitute, Opus Q4).
**ACCEPTED**: the header now lists exactly the reads at 1/2/4 and the eight enable writes at len 4, and points at
X12 for the Clear write's other widths and data.
**F11 -- stale sentences in repair 1** ("every seed above fits in ONE byte" in X8 once wide seeds landed; X9's
"returned 0" should say READ DATA 0; the brief's "59 variants against TABLE4" was really 59 ENTRIES: FIX + 49 +
nine new; "never amended" cannot be proved by a snapshot) (codex seat Q4).  **ACCEPTED**, all corrected (the brief
above uses the corrected forms).
**F12 -- the gate pins distinct-name COUNTS per family, not identities** (codex-dedicated T4): a required row
replaced by another uniquely-named passing row keeps every count.  **FILED** as a new LOW item (`fbgateidentity`):
it concerns edits to the DETECTOR, not to the product, so it is not a floor question for this round.
**F13 -- the CPU irq line can stick high** (Opus: `line10+` while disabled, then `W188=0x400`, then `line10-`
leaves the line at 1 while IRQ_STATUS reads 0 -- the enable arms call `INTERRUPT_ASSERT` without setting
`irq_asserted`, and `footbridge_interrupt_deassert` deasserts only `if (... && d->irq_asserted)`).  **FILED MED
(`fbirqstuck`)**: a real product defect in two arms #545 did not edit; its own round.
**F14 -- no guest reset-state reads** -- settled (AC.4).

**D1 -- SHIP vs DO NOT SHIP on repair 1** (agy, deepseek, minimax SHIP; codex, grok, glm, kimi-substitute DO NOT

## OPERATIONAL RULE FOR EVERY SEAT (appended by panel.sh, not by the author)

**Do not run `run.sh`, any `gate_*.sh`, or the battery.** Every measurement you need is
inlined above. A gate takes minutes -- longer than your response budget -- and a second
concurrent invocation corrupts the operator run in progress, because the gates share one
log directory. A seat that runs a gate produces no review AND may invalidate someone
else s. Building and running a single detector or mutant in your own temp directory is
fine and encouraged; driving the harness is not.

If you want a measurement that is not inlined, NAME IT and say why. Naming it is worth
far more to this panel than a timeout.

  30: 
  31: ## What #545 is (plain engineering language)
  32: 
  33: #545 changed five register arms of the DC21285 "footbridge" device model.  Before it, a guest WRITE to any of
  34: four read-only status registers (IRQ_STATUS +0x180, IRQ_RAW_STATUS +0x184, FIQ_STATUS +0x280, FIQ_RAW_STATUS
  35: +0x284) or a guest READ of the write-only IRQEnableClear (+0x18c) ended the emulator process.  After it, each
  36: such access is DECLINED -- no register changes, a declined read returns 0 -- and ANNOUNCED ONCE per site
  37: through a per-site warn-once latch (`warned_*`).  The detector is `regress/diff_footbridge.c` (an offline
  38: differential that `#include`s the real `dev_footbridge.c` and calls its access function directly) plus
  39: `regress/footbridge_sites_probe.py` (a 37-row guest probe, unchanged by this repair).
  40: 
  41: ## The chain so far (every step measured; artifacts under `regress/artifacts/fbd5/`)
  42: 
  43: 1. pass 1, pass 2, the F2 findings-sharing pass, and the flagship seat's regress/adjudication pass.
  44: 2. The flagship seat PREDICTED, by reading, that five one-condition edits to the DIRECTION test (Q1..Q5, e.g.
  45:    `if (writeflag == MEM_READ && d->irq_enable)` at IRQ_STATUS) would pass every row.  Measured TRUE:
  46:    333/333 offline and 37/37 guest for each (`q2_20260923/Q2.log`).  -> REPAIR 1 (X9 = legal x latches clear,
  47:    plus a "wide" seed state), 491 rows.
  48: 3. Repair 1's own pass 2 (panel `panel_20260923_143458` + the Opus measuring seat + a dedicated codex job):
  49:    codex, grok, glm and the kimi substitute DO NOT SHIP; agy, deepseek and minimax SHIP.  The Opus seat built
  50:    434 one-line variants of the five arms and the head predicates: **153 passed all 491 rows**.
  51: 4. That was the THIRD consecutive pass to find cells of ONE space, so the carrier's class-closure amendment
  52:    governed: REPAIR 2 enumerates the whole space from the handler's own inputs, closes it in ONE diff
  53:    (X10, X11, X12 and a fourth "ones" state), and rewrites the CONTRACT to name what is covered and what is
  54:    not.  **THIS PASS VERIFIES THAT ENUMERATION -- it is not asked to hunt new cells one at a time.**
  55:

 109: 
 110: ## C - SETTLED PREMISES (recorded resolutions; do not re-derive, but say if the diff or the handler contradicts one)
 111: 
 112: 1. **The product change is settled** -- decline + once-latched announce at the five sites, 0 for the declined
 113:    read.  Pass 1, pass 2, F2 and the adjudication all ruled on it; neither repair touches it.
 114: 2. **The floor these rows answer to** (the carrier's DEPENDS-ON rule, bounded): the predicates DOMINATING each
 115:    edited decline, walked to the function boundary, crossed with the body-read lvalues' BODY-TESTED partitions
 116:    where they are jointly reachable; a value or combination the bodies never test as a case is DISCLOSED
 117:    (clause e), not rowed.
 118: 3. **Insertion-class edits in OTHER cases** (a writer added at +0x188, the FIQ enable pair, TIMER_1_VALUE) are
 119:    outside that closure (adjudication Q1).  Their existing rows stay, ungraded as floor.
 120: 4. **No guest reset-state reads are added**: every seat that answered repair 1's Q5 agreed the offline rows carry
 121:    the floor; codex added that guest reads would buy integration coverage, not floor coverage.
 122: 5. **`fiq_status` has NO writer in the product** (grok, Opus): every FIQ status value these rows seed is synthetic.
 123:    `irq_status` is written ONLY by `footbridge_interrupt_assert` / `_deassert` (`d->irq_status |= interrupt->line`,
 124:    `d->irq_status &= ~interrupt->line`), which the offline harness stubs.
 125: 
 126: ## The handler -- `DEVICE_ACCESS(footbridge)`, the parts this closure is about (verbatim, frozen copy)

 128: NOT SHOWN: the PCI-config, UART and timer arms between `switch (relative_addr) {` and `case IRQ_STATUS:`, and the
 129: timer arms after `case FIQ_ENABLE_CLEAR:`.  They read and write no field the five arms read, EXCEPT that a timer

 952: 	/*  X8 (adjudication pass, G11): every seed in the P-rows and X1-X5 fits in ONE byte, so a legal read
 953: 	    truncated to 8 bits passed every row.  Wide seeds: each legal word read returns its full 32-bit value
 954: 	    (the masked reads: 0x80010018 & 0x00010030 = 0x00010010, 0x40020060 & 0x000200c0 = 0x00020040).  These

 990: 	    fresh instance: the access returns 1 and announces nothing; a read returns its value over the width and

   1: /*
   2:  *  Offline differential for the SHIPPED footbridge timer -- src/devices/dev_footbridge.c.
   3:  *
   4:  *  WHY THIS EXISTS.  #432 fixed a guest-reachable HOST CRASH: `random() % timer_load[i]` with
   5:  *  a guest-writable zero load is an integer division by zero, reproduced independently by two
   6:  *  seats as SIGFPE (exit 136).  A crash fix with no detector is this project's fifth vacuity
   7:  *  class, and here the detector had to be offline for a reason stronger than convenience:
   8:  *  THERE IS NO NETWINDER OR CATS RIG ANYWHERE IN regress/ -- nothing in this tree boots a
   9:  *  machine that has a footbridge.  So this is not the cheapest detector, it is the ONLY
  10:  *  possible one.
  11:  *
  12:  *  IT IS THE REPOSITORY FILE.  This driver #includes the shipped dev_footbridge.c, exactly as
  13:  *  diff_sh4_tmu.c #includes dev_sh4.c -- an identical header set, and the precedent that made
  14:  *  the feasibility question answerable at all.  Nothing is transcribed.
  15:  *
  16:  *  SEVEN STUBS, and `-Wl,--gc-sections` is LOAD-BEARING rather than cosmetic: without it the
  17:  *  link needs eleven more symbols (bus_pci_*, interrupt_*, console_start_slave,
  18:  *  machine_add_tickfunction, memory_device_register).  The trade-off, stated so it is not
  19:  *  mistaken for coverage: gc-sections drops DEVINIT, so this file cannot test devinit.
  20:  *
  21:  *  THE TWO STUBS THAT ARE INSTRUMENTS, not scaffolding: timer_add() and
  22:  *  timer_update_frequency() RECORD the frequency the device asked for.  That is what lets a
  23:  *  row assert the rate semantics directly, instead of inferring them from a boot that cannot
  24:  *  happen here.
  25:  *
  26:  *  EXPECTED VALUES ARE ABSOLUTES DERIVED FROM THE 24-BIT WIDTH, never re-read from the header
  27:  *  symbol the code itself uses.  R4's review established that a row which takes its expected
  28:  *  value from the code under test is green for ANY value of it (filed as `constblind`), so
  29:  *  16777216 is written out here on purpose.
  30:  */

  44: 
  45: bool single_step = false;
  46: bool about_to_enter_single_step = false;
  47: static int verb[16];
  48: int *debugmsg_current_verbosity = verb;
  49: 
  50: static int fatal_calls;
  51: void fatal(const char *fmt, ...) { (void) fmt; fatal_calls++; }
  52: void debug(const char *fmt, ...) { (void) fmt; }
  53: 
  54: /*  The recorder stubs: what rate did the device ASK the timer core for?  */
  55: static double last_freq = -1.0;
  56: static int timer_add_calls, timer_update_calls;
  57: struct timer;
  58: struct timer *timer_add(double freq, void (*f)(struct timer *, void *), void *extra)
  59: {
  60: 	(void) f; (void) extra;
  61: 	last_freq = freq;
  62: 	timer_add_calls++;
  63: 	return (struct timer *) (void *) &last_freq;	/*  non-NULL cookie  */
  64: }
  65: void timer_update_frequency(struct timer *t, double freq)
  66: {
  67: 	(void) t;
  68: 	last_freq = freq;
  69: 	timer_update_calls++;
  70: }
  71: 
  72: uint64_t memory_readmax64(struct cpu *cpu, unsigned char *buf, int len)
  73: {
  74: 	uint64_t x = 0; int i;
  75: 	(void) cpu;
  83: {
  84: 	int i;
  85: 	(void) cpu;
  86: 	for (i = 0; i < len; i++)
  87: 		buf[i] = (data >> (8*i)) & 255;
  88: }
  89: void console_putchar(int handle, int ch) { (void) handle; (void) ch; }
  90: 
  91: /*  The device asserts an interrupt on every delivered tick, and INTERRUPT_ASSERT calls
  92:     through a function pointer in the struct.  A calloc'd struct leaves that NULL, so the
  93:     first tick SIGSEGVs -- which looks exactly like the crash under test and would have made
  94:     this whole file a liar.  Install counting no-ops instead.  */
  95: static int irq_asserts, irq_deasserts;
  96: static void noop_assert(struct interrupt *i) { (void) i; irq_asserts++; }
  97: static void noop_deassert(struct interrupt *i) { (void) i; irq_deasserts++; }
  98: 
  99: #include "../src/devices/dev_footbridge.c"
 100: 
 101: /*  Needs the struct, so it lives AFTER the device include.  */
 102: static void arm_irqs(struct footbridge_data *d)
 103: {
 104: 	int i;
 105: 	for (i = 0; i < N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS; i++) {
 106: 		d->timer_irq[i].interrupt_assert = noop_assert;
 107: 		d->timer_irq[i].interrupt_deassert = noop_deassert;
 108: 	}
 109: }
 110: 
 111: /* --------------------------------------------------------------- harness --- */
 112: 
 113: static int rows, fails;
 114: static void check(const char *name, const char *got, const char *want)
 115: {
 116: 	rows++;
 117: 	if (strcmp(got, want) == 0)
 118: 		printf("  ok    %-54s %s\n", name, got);
 119: 	else {
 120: 		fails++;
 121: 		printf("  FAIL  %-54s\n          got  %s\n          want %s\n",
 122: 		    name, got, want);
 123: 	}
 124: }
 125: static void check_u(const char *name, uint64_t got, uint64_t want)
 126: {
 127: 	char g[32], w[32];
 128: 	snprintf(g, sizeof(g), "%" PRIu64, got);
 129: 	snprintf(w, sizeof(w), "%" PRIu64, want);
 130: 	check(name, g, w);
 131: }
 132: 
 133: /*  The helper is static inside the shipped file, which is exactly why this driver includes
 134:     the file rather than linking it.  */
 135: int main(void)
 136: {
 137: 	struct footbridge_data *d;
 138: 	struct cpu *cpu;
 139: 	struct machine *machine;
 140: 
 141: 	/*  LINE-BUFFERED ON PURPOSE.  gate_offline.sh redirects this binary's stdout to a file,
 142: 	    which makes it FULLY buffered -- so when a mutant re-introduces the crash, the log is
 143: 	    0 BYTES and the operator gets a red row with nothing in it.  Measured: line-buffered,
 144: 	    the same run leaves 943 bytes naming A1/B2/D1 as FAIL before it dies.  The gate's
 145: 	    greps fail either way, so this is legibility rather than detection -- but it is this
 146: 	    project's own recorded stdout-buffering trap, in a new place.  */
 147: 	setvbuf(stdout, NULL, _IOLBF, 0);
 148: 
 149: 	cpu = calloc(1, sizeof(struct cpu));
 150: 	machine = calloc(1, sizeof(struct machine));
 151: 	d = calloc(1, sizeof(struct footbridge_data));
 152: 	cpu->machine = machine;
 153: 	machine->emulated_hz = 100000000;	/*  100 MHz, an ordinary rate  */
 154: 
 155: 	{
 156: 		/*
 157: 		 *  SELFCHECK -- CAN THIS FILE'S COMPARATOR STILL FAIL?
 158: 		 *
 159: 		 *  Feed check() deliberate mismatches and require the failure counter to move,
 160: 		 *  then un-record them so the rows are free.  If check() has been stubbed,
 161: 		 *  neutered, or had its comparison edited away, EVERY other row in this file is
 162: 		 *  silently inert and the gate cannot tell -- measured: 118 rows across five
 163: 		 *  detectors stayed green under exactly that stub, row counts, identity rows and
 164: 		 *  verdict tokens all intact.
 165: 		 *
 166: 		 *  TWO MISMATCHES, ONE ALPHABETIC AND ONE DIGIT-LEADING, and the second is not
 167: 		 *  padding.  The first version fed only "a" vs "b", and a pass-2 seat defeated it
 168: 		 *  with one line -- `isdigit(got[0]) || strcmp(...) == 0` -- because EVERY REAL ROW
 169: 		 *  IN THIS FILE COMPARES NUMBERS.  118 rows went inert, this sentinel stayed blind,
 170: 		 *  and four of the five gate-2 self-mutants stayed green too.  A sentinel whose
 171: 		 *  input does not resemble the rows it vouches for is a sentinel with a whitelist.
 172: 		 *
 173: 		 *  THE HEALTHY PATH PRINTS AN ok-SHAPED LINE, DELIBERATELY.  The first version
 174: 		 *  printed nothing on success and a FAIL line only on failure, which broke two
 175: 		 *  things at once: there was no stable string for gate 2 to grep, so DELETING THIS
 176: 		 *  WHOLE BLOCK FROM ALL FIVE FILES left the gate at PASS with the same 220 checks
 177: 		 *  and zero red rows -- and the FAIL text it printed on the HEALTHY path polluted
 178: 		 *  the gate's kill attribution, so a self-mutant pinned to a row id like "fail"
 179: 		 *  matched this line and reported OK for a mutant nothing had caught.
 180: 		 *
 181: 		 *  `>` not `== +1`: a comparator that double-counts compares perfectly well, and
 182: 		 *  the exact form printed "no longer compares" at it.  Direction was safe, the
 183: 		 *  diagnosis was wrong.
 184: 		 *
 185: 		 *  This does NOT prove any row is correct.  It proves the comparator still fails on
 186: 		 *  inputs shaped like the ones the rows use.  A comparator that compares
 187: 		 *  SELECTIVELY -- or that special-cases this row by name -- is still invisible to
 188: 		 *  it, and only a census would see that.  Liveness sentinel, nothing more.
 189: 		 *
 190: 		 *  THE PROBE ROWS ARE NAMED @@SELFCHECK@@ SO THEY CANNOT BE MISTAKEN FOR A ROW.
 191: 		 *  They are deliberate mismatches, so check() prints FAIL for them on the HEALTHY
 192: 		 *  path -- and a pass-2 seat measured that a self-mutant pinned to a row id like
 193: 		 *  "fail" matched one of those lines and reported OK for a mutant nothing caught.
 194: 		 *  The obvious cure is worse than the disease: suppressing the print INSIDE check()
 195: 		 *  would need a name test in the comparator, which is precisely the whitelist the
 196: 		 *  same seat showed defeats this sentinel (`strncmp(name,"SELFCHECK",9)`).  So the
 197: 		 *  comparator is left alone and the probe rows carry a token no real row would use;
 198: 		 *  selfmutant.py filters it, and gate 2 greps the SUMMARY line below for presence.
 199: 		 */
 200: 		int selfcheck_f = fails, selfcheck_r = rows, selfcheck_bad = 0;
 201: 
 202: 		check("@@SELFCHECK@@/str", "a", "b");
 203: 		if (fails <= selfcheck_f)
 204: 			selfcheck_bad++;
 205: 		fails = selfcheck_f; rows = selfcheck_r;
 206: 
 207: 		check("@@SELFCHECK@@/num", "1", "2");
 208: 		if (fails <= selfcheck_f)
 209: 			selfcheck_bad++;
 210: 		fails = selfcheck_f; rows = selfcheck_r;
 211: 
 212: 		if (selfcheck_bad) {
 213: 			printf("  FAIL  SELFCHECK the comparator no longer compares (%d of 2 "
 214: 			    "mismatches went unnoticed) -- every row in this file is inert\n",
 215: 			    selfcheck_bad);
 216: 			fails = selfcheck_f + 1;
 217: 			rows = selfcheck_r;
 218: 		} else {
 219: 			printf("  ok    SELFCHECK the comparator can still fail       "
 220: 			    "str+num\n");
 221: 		}
 222: 	}
 223: 
 224: 	printf("--- A. the load interpretation: zero is a FULL PERIOD, never a divisor of 0 ---\n");
 225: 	/*  16777216 == 2^24, written as an absolute rather than read from TIMER_MAX_VAL: a row
 610: 	}
 611: 
 612: 	/*
 613: 	 *  #545 P-ROWS -- the five undefined-direction accesses that used to end the process are
 614: 	 *  DECLINED with a latched announce.  These rows pin what no stepped guest can (the guest
 615: 	 *  rows live in footbridge_sites_probe.py, arm S): PRESERVATION under a NONZERO status, the
 616: 	 *  read EXPRESSIONS, the widths, and the latch -- fatal() is a counter here, so the announce
 617: 	 *  is counted, never read (the misname check is the guest rows' job).
 618: 	 *
 619: 	 *  SEEDED BY DIRECT FIELD WRITE, NEVER THROUGH +0x188/+0x18c.  A bare calloc'd instance has
 620: 	 *  NULL d->irq callbacks and the enable arms call INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->irq), so this block
 621: 	 *  installs counting no-ops FIRST; the seeds are still written directly, so that no enable-
 622: 	 *  arm assert is mixed into the interrupt-output counts the rows below compare.
 623: 	 *  Seeds (enumerated, pass 2): 0x18/0x30/0x60/0xc0 -- every field-for-field swap in either
 624: 	 *  status expression yields a DIFFERENT value (0x50 collided: 0x50 & 0x30 == 0x18 & 0x30).
 625: 	 *  fiq_status has no writer in the product; seeding it pins the +0x280/+0x284 expressions
 626: 	 *  against a value the product never produces -- a NARROWING of that gap, not its closure.
 627: 	 */
 628: 	{
 629: 		struct footbridge_data *h = calloc(1, sizeof(struct footbridge_data));
 630: 		unsigned char buf[8];
 631: 		char nm[96], nm2[128];
 632: 		int f0, i;
 633: 		static const struct { uint64_t addr; int wf; int len; const char *what; } dec[] = {
 634: 			{ IRQ_STATUS,       MEM_WRITE, 4, "P1 write IRQ_STATUS"       },
 635: 			{ IRQ_RAW_STATUS,   MEM_WRITE, 4, "P2 write IRQ_RAW_STATUS"   },
 636: 			{ IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, MEM_READ,  4, "P3 read IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR"  },
 637: 			{ FIQ_STATUS,       MEM_WRITE, 4, "P4 write FIQ_STATUS"       },
 638: 			{ FIQ_RAW_STATUS,   MEM_WRITE, 4, "P5 write FIQ_RAW_STATUS"   },
 639: 		};
 640: 		arm_irqs(h);
 641: 		h->irq.interrupt_assert = noop_assert;
 642: 		h->irq.interrupt_deassert = noop_deassert;
 643: 		/*  seeds chosen so MASKED != RAW: a dropped `&` (raw returned as status) reds P0  */
 644: 		h->irq_status = 0x18;  h->irq_enable = 0x30;	/*  masked 0x10, raw 0x18  */
 645: 		h->fiq_status = 0x60;  h->fiq_enable = 0xc0;	/*  masked 0x40, raw 0x60  */
 646: 		fatal_calls = 0;	/*  this block counts announces from a clean baseline  */
 647: #define P_STATE_OK(tag) do { \
 648: 		snprintf(nm2, sizeof(nm2), "%s -- irq_status preserved", tag); check_u(nm2, h->irq_status, 0x18); \
 649: 		snprintf(nm2, sizeof(nm2), "%s -- irq_enable preserved", tag); check_u(nm2, h->irq_enable, 0x30); \
 650: 		snprintf(nm2, sizeof(nm2), "%s -- fiq_status preserved", tag); check_u(nm2, h->fiq_status, 0x60); \
 651: 		snprintf(nm2, sizeof(nm2), "%s -- fiq_enable preserved", tag); check_u(nm2, h->fiq_enable, 0xc0); } while (0)
 652: #define PN(suffix) (snprintf(nm, sizeof(nm), "%s: %s", dec[i].what, suffix), nm)
 653: 		/*  the legal reads FIRST, on the seeded state: the expressions, not a constant 0  */
 654: 		memset(buf, 0, sizeof(buf));
 655: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_STATUS, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
 656: 		check_u("P0 IRQ_STATUS read = irq_status & irq_enable (0x10)", memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4), 0x10);
 657: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_RAW_STATUS, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
 658: 		check_u("P0 IRQ_RAW_STATUS read = irq_status (0x18)", memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4), 0x18);
 659: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, FIQ_STATUS, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
 660: 		check_u("P0 FIQ_STATUS read = fiq_status & fiq_enable (0x40)", memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4), 0x40);
 661: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, FIQ_RAW_STATUS, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
 662: 		check_u("P0 FIQ_RAW_STATUS read = fiq_status (0x60)", memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4), 0x60);
 663: 		check_u("P0 the legal reads announce nothing", fatal_calls, 0);
 664: 		for (i = 0; i < 5; i++) {
 665: 			int a0 = irq_asserts, d0 = irq_deasserts, rv;
 666: 			f0 = fatal_calls;
 667: 			memory_writemax64(cpu, buf, 4, 0x70);	/*  bits in BOTH enables: 0x70&0x30=0x30, 0x70&0xc0=0x40  */
 668: 			rv = dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, dec[i].addr, buf, dec[i].len, dec[i].wf, h);
 669: 			check_u(PN("handler returned 1 (declined, not failed)"), rv, 1);
 670: 			check_u(PN("announced exactly once"), fatal_calls - f0, 1);
 671: 			if (dec[i].wf == MEM_READ)
 672: 				check_u(PN("the declined read returns 0"), memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4), 0);
 673: 			P_STATE_OK(PN("after #1"));
 674: 			check_u(PN("interrupt output untouched"), (irq_asserts - a0) + (irq_deasserts - d0), 0);
 675: 			f0 = fatal_calls;
 676: 			memory_writemax64(cpu, buf, 4, 0x70);
 677: 			dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, dec[i].addr, buf, dec[i].len, dec[i].wf, h);
 678: 			check_u(PN("#2 announced nothing more (latched)"), fatal_calls - f0, 0);
 679: 			memory_writemax64(cpu, buf, 4, 0x70);
 680: 			dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, dec[i].addr, buf, 1, dec[i].wf, h);
 681: 			memory_writemax64(cpu, buf, 4, 0x70);
 682: 			dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, dec[i].addr, buf, 2, dec[i].wf, h);
 683: 			check_u(PN("byte and halfword accesses: still latched"), fatal_calls - f0, 0);
 684: 			P_STATE_OK(PN("after #2, byte, halfword"));
 685: 		}
 686: 		/*  the latches are per SITE: five accesses, five announces, none shared  */
 687: 		check_u("P6 five sites announced five times in one instance", fatal_calls, 5);
 688: 		/*  a legal read after the declines still reads the seeded expressions  */
 689: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_STATUS, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
 690: 		check_u("P7 IRQ_STATUS read after the declines (0x10)", memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4), 0x10);
 691: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, FIQ_RAW_STATUS, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
 692: 		check_u("P7 FIQ_RAW_STATUS read after the declines (0x60)", memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4), 0x60);
 693: 		/*  a LEGAL EnableClear write still works, next to the declined read of the same address  */
 694: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, buf, 4, 0x10);
 695: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, buf, 4, MEM_WRITE, h);
 696: 		check_u("P8 legal EnableClear write: 0x30 & ~0x10", h->irq_enable, 0x20);
 697: 		check_u("P8 ... announced nothing", fatal_calls, 5);
 698: #undef PN
 699: #undef P_STATE_OK
 700: 		free(h);
 701: 		/*  P9: a FRESH instance whose FIRST access to a site is a BYTE write.  A decline that fires only
 702: 		    for len == 4 would let the byte fall through silently -- indistinguishable above, where the
 703: 		    word access had already latched the site.  Here it must announce.  */
 704: 		h = calloc(1, sizeof(struct footbridge_data));
 705: 		arm_irqs(h);
 706: 		h->irq.interrupt_assert = noop_assert;
 707: 		h->irq.interrupt_deassert = noop_deassert;
 708: 		h->irq_status = 0x18;  h->irq_enable = 0x30;
 709: 		fatal_calls = 0;
 710: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, buf, 1, 0x70);
 711: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_STATUS, buf, 1, MEM_WRITE, h);
 712: 		check_u("P9 a BYTE write as the first access is declined AND announced", fatal_calls, 1);
 713: 		check_u("P9 ... and stores nothing", h->irq_status, 0x18);
 714: 		free(h);
 715: 		fatal_calls = 0;
 716: 	}
 717: 
 718: 	/*  #545 pass 2 (L2 floor repair, measured by the Opus seat): the P-rows above pinned width and latch at ONE site
 719: 	    each, so a `len == 4` conjunct on any of the other four decline guards, a legal read that re-arms a latch, and
 720: 	    a decline guard widened to swallow a LEGAL narrow read all passed every row.  X1: every site at len 1 and 2 as
 721: 	    the FIRST access announces once (and the narrow declined read returns 0).  X2: legal narrow reads of the
 722: 	    five readable registers return the low lanes and announce nothing.  X3: legal reads of every sibling at every
 723: 	    width, legal enable writes, and reads of other registers re-arm NO latch.  X4/X5 (pass 2, codex): ZERO data
 724: 	    or a zero enable as the first access still declines AND announces; a legal zero Clear write does not.  */
 725: 	{
 726: 		static const struct { uint64_t addr; int wf; const char *what; } s5[] = {
 727: 			{ IRQ_STATUS, MEM_WRITE, "X1 write IRQ_STATUS" }, { IRQ_RAW_STATUS, MEM_WRITE, "X1 write IRQ_RAW_STATUS" },
 728: 			{ IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, MEM_READ, "X1 read IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR" }, { FIQ_STATUS, MEM_WRITE, "X1 write FIQ_STATUS" },
 729: 			{ FIQ_RAW_STATUS, MEM_WRITE, "X1 write FIQ_RAW_STATUS" } };
 730: 		static const struct { uint64_t addr; uint64_t want; const char *what; } rd5[] = {
 731: 			{ IRQ_STATUS, 0x10, "IRQ_STATUS" }, { IRQ_RAW_STATUS, 0x18, "IRQ_RAW_STATUS" },
 732: 			{ FIQ_STATUS, 0x40, "FIQ_STATUS" }, { FIQ_RAW_STATUS, 0x60, "FIQ_RAW_STATUS" },
 733: 			{ IRQ_ENABLE_SET, 0x30, "IRQ_ENABLE_SET" } };
 734: 		unsigned char b[8];
 735: 		char xn[160];
 736: 		int i, w, f0;
 737: 		struct footbridge_data *x;
 738: #define XSEED(x) do { arm_irqs(x); (x)->irq.interrupt_assert = noop_assert; (x)->irq.interrupt_deassert = noop_deassert; \
 739: 		(x)->irq_status = 0x18; (x)->irq_enable = 0x30; (x)->fiq_status = 0x60; (x)->fiq_enable = 0xc0; } while (0)
 740: 		for (w = 1; w <= 2; w++)
 741: 			for (i = 0; i < 5; i++) {
 742: 				x = calloc(1, sizeof(*x)); XSEED(x);
 743: 				fatal_calls = 0;
 744: 				memory_writemax64(cpu, b, w, 0x70);
 745: 				dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, s5[i].addr, b, w, s5[i].wf, x);
 746: 				snprintf(xn, sizeof(xn), "%s: len %d as the FIRST access announces once", s5[i].what, w);
 747: 				check_u(xn, fatal_calls, 1);
 748: 				if (s5[i].wf == MEM_READ) {	/*  the narrow declined read's VALUE (pass 2)  */
 749: 					snprintf(xn, sizeof(xn), "X1 read IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR: len %d declined read returns 0", w);
 750: 					check_u(xn, memory_readmax64(cpu, b, w), 0);
 751: 				}
 752: 				free(x);
 753: 			}
 754: 		x = calloc(1, sizeof(*x)); XSEED(x);
 755: 		fatal_calls = 0;
 756: 		for (w = 1; w <= 2; w++)
 757: 			for (i = 0; i < 5; i++) {
 758: 				memset(b, 0, sizeof(b));
 759: 				dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, rd5[i].addr, b, w, MEM_READ, x);
 760: 				snprintf(xn, sizeof(xn), "X2 legal len %d read of %s = low lanes", w, rd5[i].what);
 761: 				check_u(xn, memory_readmax64(cpu, b, w), rd5[i].want & (w == 1 ? 0xff : 0xffff));
 762: 			}
 763: 		check_u("X2 the legal narrow reads announce nothing", fatal_calls, 0);
 764: 		for (i = 0; i < 5; i++) {
 765: 			memory_writemax64(cpu, b, 4, 0x70);
 766: 			dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, s5[i].addr, b, 4, s5[i].wf, x);
 767: 		}
 768: 		check_u("X3 the five declines latch (5 announces)", fatal_calls, 5);
 769: 		f0 = fatal_calls;
 770: 		for (w = 1; w <= 4; w *= 2)
 771: 			for (i = 0; i < 5; i++)
 772: 				dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, rd5[i].addr, b, w, MEM_READ, x);
 773: 		for (i = 0; i < 5; i++) {
 774: 			memory_writemax64(cpu, b, 4, 0x70);
 775: 			dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, s5[i].addr, b, 4, s5[i].wf, x);
 776: 		}
 777: 		check_u("X3 legal reads of every sibling re-arm NO latch", fatal_calls - f0, 0);
 778: 		/*  X3, widened (pass 2): legal WRITES to the four enable registers, and reads of registers outside the
 779: 		    five above (VENDOR_ID, TIMER_1_VALUE, the silent FIQEnableClear read), re-arm no latch either.  */
 780: 		f0 = fatal_calls;
 781: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, b, 4, 0);
 782: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_ENABLE_SET, b, 4, MEM_WRITE, x);
 783: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, b, 4, MEM_WRITE, x);
 784: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, FIQ_ENABLE_SET, b, 4, MEM_WRITE, x);
 785: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, FIQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, b, 4, MEM_WRITE, x);
 786: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, VENDOR_ID, b, 4, MEM_READ, x);
 787: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_VALUE, b, 4, MEM_READ, x);
 788: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, FIQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, b, 4, MEM_READ, x);
 789: 		for (i = 0; i < 5; i++) {
 790: 			memory_writemax64(cpu, b, 4, 0x70);
 791: 			dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, s5[i].addr, b, 4, s5[i].wf, x);
 792: 		}

 382: /*
 383:  *  footbridge_interrupt_assert():
 384:  */
 385: void footbridge_interrupt_assert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
 386: {
 387: 	struct footbridge_data *d = (struct footbridge_data *) interrupt->extra;
 388: 	d->irq_status |= interrupt->line;
 389: 
 390: 	// Patch from NetBSD:
 391: 	// "fix hang interrupt issue. Always assert the irq's even if we have
 392: 	//  already asserted the global irq for the device. prevents hangs with
 393: 	//  cats."
 394: 	if ((d->irq_status & d->irq_enable) /* && !d->irq_asserted */) {
 395: 		d->irq_asserted = 1;
 396: 		INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->irq);
 397: 	}
 398: }
 399: 
 400: 
 401: /*
 402:  *  footbridge_interrupt_deassert():
 403:  */
 404: void footbridge_interrupt_deassert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
 405: {
 406: 	struct footbridge_data *d = (struct footbridge_data *) interrupt->extra;
 407: 	d->irq_status &= ~interrupt->line;
 408: 
 409: 	if (!(d->irq_status & d->irq_enable) && d->irq_asserted) {
 410: 		d->irq_asserted = 0;
 411: 		INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->irq);
 412: 	}
 413: }

 779: 		    five above (VENDOR_ID, TIMER_1_VALUE, the silent FIQEnableClear read), re-arm no latch either.  */
 780: 		f0 = fatal_calls;
 781: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, b, 4, 0);
 782: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_ENABLE_SET, b, 4, MEM_WRITE, x);
 783: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, b, 4, MEM_WRITE, x);
 784: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, FIQ_ENABLE_SET, b, 4, MEM_WRITE, x);
 785: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, FIQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, b, 4, MEM_WRITE, x);
 786: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, VENDOR_ID, b, 4, MEM_READ, x);
 787: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_VALUE, b, 4, MEM_READ, x);
 788: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, FIQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, b, 4, MEM_READ, x);
 789: 		for (i = 0; i < 5; i++) {
 790: 			memory_writemax64(cpu, b, 4, 0x70);
 791: 			dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, s5[i].addr, b, 4, s5[i].wf, x);
 792: 		}
 793: 		check_u("X3 legal ZERO-data enable writes and other-register reads re-arm NO latch", fatal_calls - f0, 0);
 794: 		free(x);
 795: 		/*  X4 (codex, pass 2): the FIRST access carries ZERO -- a zero-data write, or the +0x18c read with
 796: 		    IRQEnable 0.  A latch guard conditioned on the data (`&& idata`), a +0x18c guard conditioned on
 797: 		    irq_enable, or a direction test rewritten as `!idata`, lets such an access through without an
 798: 		    announce; each must announce once and change no field.  (A WRITE-site guard conditioned on an
 799: 		    enable -- X4 keeps the seeded enables there -- is X6's all-zero-state cells, not X4's.)  */
 800: 		for (i = 0; i < 5; i++) {
 801: 			x = calloc(1, sizeof(*x)); XSEED(x);
 802: 			if (s5[i].wf == MEM_READ)
 803: 				x->irq_enable = 0;
 804: 			fatal_calls = 0;
 805: 			memory_writemax64(cpu, b, 4, 0);
 806: 			dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, s5[i].addr, b, 4, s5[i].wf, x);
 807: 			snprintf(xn, sizeof(xn), "X4 %s: ZERO data/enable as the FIRST access announces once", s5[i].what + 3);
 808: 			check_u(xn, fatal_calls, 1);
 809: 			snprintf(xn, sizeof(xn), "X4 %s: ZERO data/enable changes no field", s5[i].what + 3);
 810: 			check_u(xn, (x->irq_status == 0x18) + (x->irq_enable == (s5[i].wf == MEM_READ ? 0u : 0x30u))
 811: 			    + (x->fiq_status == 0x60) + (x->fiq_enable == 0xc0), 4);
 812: 			free(x);
 813: 		}
 814: 		/*  X5 (codex, pass 2): a LEGAL ZERO EnableClear write is a write, not a declined read -- a direction test
 815: 		    rewritten as `idata` sends it to the decline and announces.  */
 816: 		x = calloc(1, sizeof(*x)); XSEED(x);
 817: 		fatal_calls = 0;
 818: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, b, 4, 0);
 819: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, b, 4, MEM_WRITE, x);
 820: 		check_u("X5 a legal ZERO EnableClear write announces nothing", fatal_calls, 0);
 821: 		check_u("X5 ... and leaves IRQEnable at 0x30", x->irq_enable, 0x30);
 822: #undef XSEED
 823: 		free(x);
 824: 		fatal_calls = 0;
 825: 	}
 826: 
 827: 	/*
 828: 	 *  #545 CLASS CLOSURE (repair pass 2 -- two consecutive passes found breaches of ONE class, so the
 829: 	 *  whole space is enumerated from the handler's own inputs and closed in one block; carrier rule).
 830: 	 *
 831: 	 *  X6: EVERY DECLINED ACCESS -- site (5) x width (1, 2, 4) x data (zero, nonzero; writes only) x state
 832: 	 *      (seeded, all-zero, wide, ones) x occurrence (first, repeated).  Each cell, as ONE row, requires: the handler
 833: 	 *      returns 1; the announce count moves by exactly 1 on the first access and 0 on the repeat; all four
 834: 	 *      register fields are unchanged; the interrupt output is untouched (no assert, no deassert); and a
 835: 	 *      declined read returns 0 over its whole width.
 836: 	 *  X7: EVERY LEGAL ACCESS RE-ARMS NO LATCH -- with all five sites latched: each enable write (Set/Clear,
 837: 	 *      IRQ/FIQ) with zero and nonzero data, and each legal read of the readable registers plus VENDOR_ID
 838: 	 *      and TIMER_1_VALUE at widths 1, 2 and 4; from the seeded, all-zero, wide and ones states (so a read
 839: 	 *      that returns 0 is covered, and so is every bit of every field).  After each, re-declining all five
 840: 	 *      sites must announce nothing.  X7 observes the ANNOUNCE channel only; the value channel of a legal
 841: 	 *      access with the latches set is X10's.
 842: 	 *  X9-X12 (below X8) are the rest of the legal side: X9 with the latches still CLEAR, X10 with every latch
 843: 	 *  SET (value and effect), X11 the cross-site latch ORDER, X12 the legal Clear write's width and data.
 844: 	 *
 845: 	 *  CONTRACT (clause e).  THE SPACE, enumerated from the handler: the case label (the timer fold maps no
 846: 	 *  address onto these five), the direction test, the width, idata (read by the legal Clear write), the four
 847: 	 *  interrupt fields (read by the legal branches) and the five latches (each read by its own decline).  The
 848: 	 *  bodies TEST only the direction, their own latch, and (irq_status & the new enable) in the Clear write;
 849: 	 *  every other input is a value they compute with.  COVERED: declined x width x data {0, 0x70} x state x
 850: 	 *  first/repeat (X6); legal x width x state x own latch clear (X9) and every latch set (X7 announce, X10
 851: 	 *  value and effect); each ordered pair of sites for the first decline (X11); the legal Clear write at every
 852: 	 *  width and with all-ones data (X9, X12).  STATES: seeded, all-zero, wide and ONES -- with all-zero, the
 853: 	 *  ones state makes every single-bit condition on one field divert in one of the two.
 854: 	 *  *** THIS SAID "the cells above are the whole class" and was measured false TWICE on 2026-09-23: first the
 855: 	 *  reset state (legal x all-zero x latches clear -- five one-conjunct edits to the DIRECTION test passed all
 856: 	 *  333 rows and all 37 guest rows, q2_20260923/Q2.log), then, after X9 closed that, the value channel with
 857: 	 *  the latches set, the cross-site order, the narrow Clear write and any field bit no state set (the Opus
 858: 	 *  seat's 434 variants, _scratchpad/fbd5/q2r/opus/; a row set it built and measured is X10-X12). ***
 859: 	 *  NOT COVERED, stated so a later escape is "outside the contract", not a surprise: a condition on one EXACT
 860: 	 *  field value (`d->irq_enable != 0x10`) -- the bodies test no values and no finite set of states holds them
 861: 	 *  all; the ADDRESS axis -- a case label added for another offset, or a widened timer fold, aliasing an
 862: 	 *  address onto an arm; joint conditions over two fields beyond the four states, or over two latches beyond
 863: 	 *  X11's pairs; WIDE DATA on a declined write (data stays 0 / 0x70); the interrupt output after a legal
 864: 	 *  access except where X9/X10/X12 pin it; any other register's semantics; and a latch guard conditioned on
 865: 	 *  a field neither branch reads (measured F1: `&& !timer_control[0]` passes every row here; the guest sites
 866: 	 *  probe catches it only because its prologue arms timer 1; table4_20260919).  fiq_status has NO writer in
 867: 	 *  the product, so every FIQ status value these rows seed is synthetic.
 868: 	 */
 869: 	{
 870: 		static const struct { uint64_t addr; int wf; const char *nm; } S5[] = {
 871: 			{ IRQ_STATUS, MEM_WRITE, "write IRQ_STATUS" }, { IRQ_RAW_STATUS, MEM_WRITE, "write IRQ_RAW_STATUS" },
 872: 			{ IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, MEM_READ, "read IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR" }, { FIQ_STATUS, MEM_WRITE, "write FIQ_STATUS" },
 873: 			{ FIQ_RAW_STATUS, MEM_WRITE, "write FIQ_RAW_STATUS" } };
 874: 		/*  #545 adjudication (Fable 5.1, Q2 (e) cell): the byte seeds left bits 8-31 of every field unexercised,
 875: 		    so a decline store or a latch clearer keyed on them passed every row.  "wide" is X8's seeds -- and it
 876: 		    still left byte 1 of every field, bit 0, bits 18-29 and byte 3 of the enables at 0, so a condition keyed
 877: 		    on one of those bits passed every row (repair pass 2, five seats; Opus measured 30 such edits green).
 878: 		    "ones" sets every bit of every field: with all-zero, a single-bit condition diverts in one of the two.  */
 879: 		static const struct { uint32_t is, ie, fs, fe; const char *nm; } ST[4] = {
 880: 			{ 0x18, 0x30, 0x60, 0xc0, "seeded" }, { 0, 0, 0, 0, "all-zero" },
 881: 			{ 0x80010018, 0x00010030, 0x40020060, 0x000200c0, "wide" },
 882: 			{ 0xffffffff, 0xffffffff, 0xffffffff, 0xffffffff, "ones" } };
 883: 		static const int W[3] = { 1, 2, 4 };
 884: 		static const struct { uint64_t addr; int wf; uint64_t data; const char *nm; } LW[8] = {
 885: 			{ IRQ_ENABLE_SET, MEM_WRITE, 0, "IRQ Set write 0" }, { IRQ_ENABLE_SET, MEM_WRITE, 0x70, "IRQ Set write 0x70" },
 886: 			{ IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, MEM_WRITE, 0, "IRQ Clear write 0" }, { IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, MEM_WRITE, 0x70, "IRQ Clear write 0x70" },
 887: 			{ FIQ_ENABLE_SET, MEM_WRITE, 0, "FIQ Set write 0" }, { FIQ_ENABLE_SET, MEM_WRITE, 0x70, "FIQ Set write 0x70" },
 888: 			{ FIQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, MEM_WRITE, 0, "FIQ Clear write 0" }, { FIQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, MEM_WRITE, 0x70, "FIQ Clear write 0x70" } };
 889: 		static const struct { uint64_t addr; const char *nm; } LR[9] = {
 890: 			{ IRQ_STATUS, "IRQ_STATUS" }, { IRQ_RAW_STATUS, "IRQ_RAW_STATUS" }, { FIQ_STATUS, "FIQ_STATUS" },
 891: 			{ FIQ_RAW_STATUS, "FIQ_RAW_STATUS" }, { IRQ_ENABLE_SET, "IRQ_ENABLE_SET" }, { FIQ_ENABLE_SET, "FIQ_ENABLE_SET" },
 892: 			{ FIQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, "FIQ_ENABLE_CLEAR" }, { VENDOR_ID, "VENDOR_ID" }, { TIMER_1_VALUE, "TIMER_1_VALUE" } };
 893: 		unsigned char b[8];
 894: 		char xn[200];
 895: 		int si, st, wi, dv, ord, k, f0, a0, d0, rv, ok;
 896: 		struct footbridge_data *x;
 897: #define XSET(x, s) do { arm_irqs(x); (x)->irq.interrupt_assert = noop_assert; (x)->irq.interrupt_deassert = noop_deassert; \
 898: 		(x)->irq_status = ST[s].is; (x)->irq_enable = ST[s].ie; (x)->fiq_status = ST[s].fs; (x)->fiq_enable = ST[s].fe; } while (0)
 899: #define XSAME(x, s) ((x)->irq_status == ST[s].is && (x)->irq_enable == ST[s].ie && (x)->fiq_status == ST[s].fs \
 900: 		&& (x)->fiq_enable == ST[s].fe)
 901: 		for (si = 0; si < 5; si++)
 902: 			for (st = 0; st < 4; st++)
 903: 				for (wi = 0; wi < 3; wi++)
 904: 					for (dv = 0; dv < (S5[si].wf == MEM_WRITE ? 2 : 1); dv++) {
 905: 						x = calloc(1, sizeof(*x)); XSET(x, st);
 906: 						fatal_calls = 0;
 907: 						for (ord = 0; ord < 2; ord++) {
 908: 							a0 = irq_asserts; d0 = irq_deasserts; f0 = fatal_calls;
 909: 							memset(b, 0xa5, sizeof(b));
 910: 							if (S5[si].wf == MEM_WRITE)
 911: 								memory_writemax64(cpu, b, W[wi], dv ? 0x70 : 0);
 912: 							rv = dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, S5[si].addr, b, W[wi], S5[si].wf, x);
 913: 							ok = rv == 1 && fatal_calls - f0 == (ord == 0 ? 1 : 0) && XSAME(x, st)
 914: 							    && irq_asserts == a0 && irq_deasserts == d0
 915: 							    && (S5[si].wf != MEM_READ || memory_readmax64(cpu, b, W[wi]) == 0);
 916: 							snprintf(xn, sizeof(xn), "X6 %s len %d %s %s %s: declined, fields/irq kept, once",
 917: 							    S5[si].nm, W[wi], S5[si].wf == MEM_WRITE ? (dv ? "data 0x70" : "data 0") : "",
 918: 							    ST[st].nm, ord ? "repeat" : "first");
 919: 							check_u(xn, ok, 1);
 920: 						}
 921: 						free(x);
 922: 					}
 923: 		for (st = 0; st < 4; st++)
 924: 			for (k = 0; k < 8 + 9 * 3; k++) {
 925: 				x = calloc(1, sizeof(*x)); XSET(x, st);
 926: 				for (si = 0; si < 5; si++) {
 927: 					memory_writemax64(cpu, b, 4, 0x70);
 928: 					dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, S5[si].addr, b, 4, S5[si].wf, x);
 929: 				}
 930: 				f0 = fatal_calls;
 931: 				if (k < 8) {
 932: 					memory_writemax64(cpu, b, 4, LW[k].data);
 933: 					dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, LW[k].addr, b, 4, MEM_WRITE, x);
 934: 					snprintf(xn, sizeof(xn), "X7 %s (%s) re-arms NO latch", LW[k].nm, ST[st].nm);
 935: 				} else {
 936: 					int r = (k - 8) / 3, w = W[(k - 8) % 3];
 937: 					memset(b, 0, sizeof(b));
 938: 					dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, LR[r].addr, b, w, MEM_READ, x);
 939: 					snprintf(xn, sizeof(xn), "X7 legal len %d read of %s (%s) re-arms NO latch", w, LR[r].nm, ST[st].nm);
 940: 				}
 941: 				for (si = 0; si < 5; si++) {
 942: 					memory_writemax64(cpu, b, 4, 0x70);
 943: 					dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, S5[si].addr, b, 4, S5[si].wf, x);
 944: 				}
 945: 				check_u(xn, fatal_calls - f0, 0);
 946: 				free(x);
 947: 			}
 948: #undef XSAME
 949: #undef XSET
 950: 		fatal_calls = 0;
 951: 	}
 952: 	/*  X8 (adjudication pass, G11): every seed in the P-rows and X1-X5 fits in ONE byte, so a legal read

 exited 1 in 1394ms:
 953: 	    truncated to 8 bits passed every row.  Wide seeds: each legal word read returns its full 32-bit value
 954: 	    (the masked reads: 0x80010018 & 0x00010030 = 0x00010010, 0x40020060 & 0x000200c0 = 0x00020040).  These
 955: 	    seeds set bits in bytes 0, 2 and 3 only; the ONES state in X6/X7/X9-X12 is what exercises byte 1.  */
 956: 	{
 957: 		struct footbridge_data *x = calloc(1, sizeof(*x));
 958: 		unsigned char b[8];
 959: 		arm_irqs(x);
 960: 		x->irq.interrupt_assert = noop_assert;
 961: 		x->irq.interrupt_deassert = noop_deassert;
 962: 		x->irq_status = 0x80010018;  x->irq_enable = 0x00010030;
 963: 		x->fiq_status = 0x40020060;  x->fiq_enable = 0x000200c0;
 964: 		fatal_calls = 0;
 965: 		memset(b, 0, sizeof(b));
 966: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_STATUS, b, 4, MEM_READ, x);
 967: 		check_u("X8 wide IRQ_STATUS read = 0x00010010 (all 32 bits)", memory_readmax64(cpu, b, 4), 0x00010010);
 968: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_RAW_STATUS, b, 4, MEM_READ, x);
 969: 		check_u("X8 wide IRQ_RAW_STATUS read = 0x80010018", memory_readmax64(cpu, b, 4), 0x80010018);
 970: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, FIQ_STATUS, b, 4, MEM_READ, x);
 971: 		check_u("X8 wide FIQ_STATUS read = 0x00020040", memory_readmax64(cpu, b, 4), 0x00020040);
 972: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, FIQ_RAW_STATUS, b, 4, MEM_READ, x);
 973: 		check_u("X8 wide FIQ_RAW_STATUS read = 0x40020060", memory_readmax64(cpu, b, 4), 0x40020060);
 974: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_ENABLE_SET, b, 4, MEM_READ, x);
 975: 		check_u("X8 wide IRQEnable read = 0x00010030", memory_readmax64(cpu, b, 4), 0x00010030);
 976: 		check_u("X8 the wide legal reads announce nothing", fatal_calls, 0);
 977: 		free(x);
 978: 		fatal_calls = 0;
 979: 	}
 980: 	/*  X9 (adjudication pass, 2026-09-23 -- Fable 5.1's Q2, MEASURED before this block existed): the legal
 981: 	    accesses of the five sites -- reads of the four status registers and of IRQEnable (+0x188) at widths 1, 2
 982: 	    and 4, and the eight enable writes at len 4 (X12 has the Clear write's other widths and data) -- WITH THE
 983: 	    LATCHES STILL CLEAR, from each state.  X7 latched all five sites before its legal access,
 984: 	    so a direction test conjoined with a field that is 0 in the RESET state (`writeflag == MEM_READ &&
 985: 	    d->irq_enable` at IRQ_STATUS, its three read mirrors, `writeflag == MEM_WRITE && d->irq_enable` at
 986: 	    IRQEnableClear) sent the legal access into the decline, which announced a phantom complaint, consumed the
 987: 	    latch and -- for the reads -- returned read data 0, the right value in that state; the diverted Clear WRITE
 988: 	    cleared nothing, which in that state is also the right result -- and every row stayed green
 989: 	    (regress/artifacts/fbd5/q2_20260923/Q2.log: 333/333 and 37/37 for all five).  Each cell is ONE row on a
 990: 	    fresh instance: the access returns 1 and announces nothing; a read returns its value over the width and
 991: 	    changes no field; a write sets the enable it targets as written and leaves the other three alone, and IRQ
 992: 	    Set/Clear drive the interrupt output exactly once by (irq_status & the new enable) while FIQ Set/Clear do
 993: 	    not touch it; then the five declines announce EXACTLY 5, because no latch was consumed.  */
 994: 	{
 995: 		static const struct { uint32_t is, ie, fs, fe; const char *nm; } S9[4] = {
 996: 			{ 0x18, 0x30, 0x60, 0xc0, "seeded" }, { 0, 0, 0, 0, "all-zero" },
 997: 			{ 0x80010018, 0x00010030, 0x40020060, 0x000200c0, "wide" },
 998: 			{ 0xffffffff, 0xffffffff, 0xffffffff, 0xffffffff, "ones" } };
 999: 		static const struct { uint64_t addr; int wf; } D5[5] = {
1000: 			{ IRQ_STATUS, MEM_WRITE }, { IRQ_RAW_STATUS, MEM_WRITE }, { IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, MEM_READ },
1001: 			{ FIQ_STATUS, MEM_WRITE }, { FIQ_RAW_STATUS, MEM_WRITE } };
1002: 		static const struct { uint64_t addr; const char *nm; } R5[5] = {
1003: 			{ IRQ_STATUS, "IRQ_STATUS" }, { IRQ_RAW_STATUS, "IRQ_RAW_STATUS" }, { FIQ_STATUS, "FIQ_STATUS" },
1004: 			{ FIQ_RAW_STATUS, "FIQ_RAW_STATUS" }, { IRQ_ENABLE_SET, "IRQEnable" } };
1005: 		static const struct { uint64_t addr; int fiq, set; uint32_t data; const char *nm; } W8[8] = {
1006: 			{ IRQ_ENABLE_SET, 0, 1, 0, "IRQ Set write 0" }, { IRQ_ENABLE_SET, 0, 1, 0x70, "IRQ Set write 0x70" },
1007: 			{ IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, 0, 0, 0, "IRQ Clear write 0" }, { IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, 0, 0, 0x70, "IRQ Clear write 0x70" },
1008: 			{ FIQ_ENABLE_SET, 1, 1, 0, "FIQ Set write 0" }, { FIQ_ENABLE_SET, 1, 1, 0x70, "FIQ Set write 0x70" },
1009: 			{ FIQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, 1, 0, 0, "FIQ Clear write 0" }, { FIQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, 1, 0, 0x70, "FIQ Clear write 0x70" } };
1010: 		static const int W9[3] = { 1, 2, 4 };
1011: 		unsigned char b[8];
1012: 		char xn[200];
1013: 		int st, k, si, a0, d0, rv, ok;
1014: 		struct footbridge_data *x;
1015: 		for (st = 0; st < 4; st++)
1016: 			for (k = 0; k < 5 * 3 + 8; k++) {
1017: 				uint32_t is = S9[st].is, ie = S9[st].ie, fs = S9[st].fs, fe = S9[st].fe;
1018: 				x = calloc(1, sizeof(*x));
1019: 				arm_irqs(x);
1020: 				x->irq.interrupt_assert = noop_assert;
1021: 				x->irq.interrupt_deassert = noop_deassert;
1022: 				x->irq_status = is;  x->irq_enable = ie;  x->fiq_status = fs;  x->fiq_enable = fe;
1023: 				fatal_calls = 0;
1024: 				a0 = irq_asserts;  d0 = irq_deasserts;
1025: 				if (k < 15) {
1026: 					int r = k / 3, w = W9[k % 3];
1027: 					uint64_t want = r == 0 ? (uint64_t) (is & ie) : r == 1 ? (uint64_t) is
1028: 					    : r == 2 ? (uint64_t) (fs & fe) : r == 3 ? (uint64_t) fs : (uint64_t) ie;
1029: 					want &= w == 4 ? 0xffffffffULL : w == 2 ? 0xffffULL : 0xffULL;
1030: 					memset(b, 0xa5, sizeof(b));
1031: 					rv = dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, R5[r].addr, b, w, MEM_READ, x);
1032: 					ok = rv == 1 && fatal_calls == 0 && memory_readmax64(cpu, b, w) == want
1033: 					    && x->irq_status == is && x->irq_enable == ie && x->fiq_status == fs
1034: 					    && x->fiq_enable == fe && irq_asserts == a0 && irq_deasserts == d0;
1035: 					snprintf(xn, sizeof(xn), "X9 legal len %d read of %s (%s, latches clear)",
1036: 					    w, R5[r].nm, S9[st].nm);
1037: 				} else {
1038: 					int j = k - 15;
1039: 					uint32_t old = W8[j].fiq ? fe : ie;
1040: 					uint32_t ne = W8[j].set ? (old | W8[j].data) : (old & ~W8[j].data);
1041: 					memory_writemax64(cpu, b, 4, W8[j].data);
1042: 					rv = dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, W8[j].addr, b, 4, MEM_WRITE, x);
1043: 					ok = rv == 1 && fatal_calls == 0 && x->irq_status == is && x->fiq_status == fs
1044: 					    && (W8[j].fiq ? (x->fiq_enable == ne && x->irq_enable == ie)
1045: 					                  : (x->irq_enable == ne && x->fiq_enable == fe))
1046: 					    && (W8[j].fiq ? (irq_asserts == a0 && irq_deasserts == d0)
1047: 					        : (is & ne) ? (irq_asserts == a0 + 1 && irq_deasserts == d0)

 350: 			c->timer_load[0] = 50000;	/*  an ordinary legal timer  */
 351: 			c->timer_control[0] = TIMER_ENABLE;
 352: 			for (i = 0; i < 400; i++)
 353: 				c->pending_timer_interrupts[0]++;
 354: 			c->timer_load[0] = 0;		/*  the guest writes zero  */
 355: 			dev_footbridge_tick(cpu, c);
 356: 			_exit(0);
 357: 		} else {
 358: 			int st = 0;
 359: 			waitpid(pid, &st, 0);
 360: 			check("F1 a zero load after a backlog does not kill the host",
 361: 			    WIFEXITED(st) && WEXITSTATUS(st) == 0 ? "survived"
 362: 			    : "KILLED BY SIGNAL", "survived");
 363: 		}
 364: 	}
 365: 
 366: 	printf("--- G. the CONTROL write path: a legal encoding must not exit() ---\n");
 367: 	/*  THE ROW THAT DEFENDS THE FOLD.  Section C proves the helper INTERPRETS 0x0C; this
 368: 	    proves the guest can actually WRITE it.  Before #432 this exact write reached
 369: 	    fatal()+exit(1) in the CONTROL arm -- a guest selecting the external clock source
 370: 	    killed the host, and no amount of helper correctness would have saved it.  Forked,
 371: 	    because a mutant that restores the exit(1) would otherwise take the gate binary
 372: 	    down and score as a census FAULT instead of a named-row KILL.  */
 373: 	{
 374: 		pid_t pid = fork();
 375: 		if (pid == 0) {
 376: 			struct footbridge_data *c = calloc(1, sizeof(*c));
 377: 			unsigned char buf[4];
 378: 			arm_irqs(c);
 379: 			c->timer_load[0] = 1000;
 380: 			memset(buf, 0, sizeof(buf));
 381: 			buf[0] = TIMER_ENABLE | TIMER_FCLK_16 | TIMER_FCLK_256;
 382: 			dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_CONTROL, buf,
 383: 			    4, MEM_WRITE, c);
 384: 			_exit(0);
 385: 		} else {
 386: 			int st = 0;
 387: 			waitpid(pid, &st, 0);
 388: 			check("G1 a guest write of ENABLE|EXTERNAL does not exit()",
 389: 			    WIFEXITED(st) && WEXITSTATUS(st) == 0 ? "survived"
 390: 			    : "HOST KILLED", "survived");
 391: 		}
 392: 	}
 393: 
 394: 	printf("--- H. the LOAD register reads back EXACTLY what the guest wrote ---\n");
 395: 	/*  THE FIFTH-VACUITY-CLASS ROW, and the adjudicating seat found it by attacking its own
 396: 	    design: "timer_load[] is never rewritten -- the guest reads back exactly what it
 397: 	    wrote" is a shipped, load-bearing claim, and it had NO DETECTOR.  Reinstating
 398: 	    normalize-at-write -- `timer_load = idata ? idata : 1`, precisely the alternative
 399: 	    this round REJECTED -- passed all fifteen rows, because nothing in this file ever
 400: 	    drove TIMER_1_LOAD through the access handler; every other row pokes struct fields
 401: 	    directly.  These two rows go through dev_footbridge_access in both directions.  */
 402: 	{
 403: 		struct footbridge_data *h = calloc(1, sizeof(*h));
 404: 		unsigned char buf[4];
 405: 		uint64_t back;
 406: 		arm_irqs(h);
 407: 
 408: 		memset(buf, 0, sizeof(buf));
 409: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_LOAD, buf, 4, MEM_WRITE, h);
 410: 		memset(buf, 0xff, sizeof(buf));
 411: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_LOAD, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
 412: 		back = memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4);
 413: 		check_u("H1 a guest write of 0 reads back as 0, not normalised", back, 0);
 414: 
 415: 		/*  H2 pins the 24-bit MASK as well: 0x1000000 is "one full wrap", and the mask
 416: 		    aliases it to zero -- which is the arithmetic that makes the full-period
 417: 		    mapping the continuous choice in the first place.  */
 418: 		memset(buf, 0, sizeof(buf));
 419: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, buf, 4, 0x1000000);
 420: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_LOAD, buf, 4, MEM_WRITE, h);
 421: 		memset(buf, 0xff, sizeof(buf));
 422: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_LOAD, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
 423: 		back = memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4);
 424: 		check_u("H2 0x1000000 is masked to 0, and stays 0 on read-back", back, 0);
 425: 		free(h);
 426: 	}
 427: 
 428: 	/*  fbclearread: `case TIMER_1_CLEAR:` carries NO writeflag test, while the case
 429: 	    immediately above it (TIMER_1_CONTROL) does.  So a guest READ runs the whole
 430: 	    body: it reloads the counter when periodic, decrements the pending count, and
 431: 	    DEASSERTS the interrupt.  A guest that merely reads the register silently
 432: 	    acknowledges a pending timer interrupt -- the likely guest-visible consequence
 433: 	    is a LOST interrupt, not a corrupted value.
 434: 
 435: 	    FOUR addresses, not one: the timer fold normalises 0x30C/0x32C/0x34C/0x36C onto
 436: 	    this single case.
 437: 
 438: 	    These rows drive the ACCESS HANDLER rather than poking struct fields, for the
 439: 	    reason this file already records above: a struct-poking row cannot see a
 440: 	    handler-level defect at all.  */
 441: 	{
 442: 		struct footbridge_data *h = calloc(1, sizeof(*h));
 443: 		unsigned char buf[4];
 444: 		uint64_t back;
 445: 
 446: 		arm_irqs(h);
 447: 
 448: 		/*  CONTROL first: a WRITE must decrement.  Without it a green defect row
 449: 		    could equally mean the handler never ran.  */
 450: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[0] = 2;
 451: 		memset(buf, 0, sizeof(buf));
 452: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_CLEAR, buf, 4, MEM_WRITE, h);
 453: 		check_u("[LIVE] a WRITE of TIMER_1_CLEAR decrements pending",
 454: 		    h->pending_timer_interrupts[0], 1);
 455: 
 456: 		/*  THE DEFECT: a READ must leave the pending count alone.  */
 457: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[0] = 2;
 458: 		memset(buf, 0xff, sizeof(buf));
 459: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_CLEAR, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
 460: 		/*  THE GUARD WRAPS THREE EFFECTS AND THE ROWS ABOVE OBSERVE TWO.
 461: 		    reload_timer_value and the pending decrement are both pinned;
 462: 		    INTERRUPT_DEASSERT was not.  A panel seat named the mutant that
 463: 		    exploits the gap -- hoist the deassert OUT of the guard so a READ
 464: 		    still deasserts:
 465: 
 466: 		        if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) { reload; pending--; }
 467: 		        INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->timer_irq[timer_nr]);
 468: 
 469: 		    Every other row passes -- the deassert touches neither the pending
 470: 		    count nor timer_value -- while the guest READ once again
 471: 		    acknowledges a pending interrupt.  **That is #474's headline
 472: 		    symptom, reintroduced**: the round's own record calls the
 473: 		    consequence a LOST interrupt, and a lost interrupt IS a spurious
 474: 		    deassert.
 475: 
 476: 		    The counter was already armed by noop_deassert and NEVER READ.
 477: 		    A counter nothing asserts on is a row that does not exist.  */
 478: 		/*  THE ACCEPT COMPANION, and the row below is HALF A ROW without it.
 479: 		    A row that wants ZERO can only fail UPWARD: delete
 480: 		    INTERRUPT_DEASSERT from the guarded body outright and the READ
 481: 		    row stays green, because zero deasserts is exactly what it
 482: 		    asserts.  Measured by a panel seat against the version that
 483: 		    shipped this row without its pair.
 484: 
 485: 		    This is the de-escalation clause's accept side in miniature:
 486: 		    the fix made a channel CONDITIONAL, so the detector owes a row
 487: 		    proving the channel still fires on the legal path.  */
 488: 		irq_deasserts = 0;
 489: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[0] = 2;
 490: 		memset(buf, 0, sizeof(buf));
 491: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_CLEAR, buf, 4, MEM_WRITE, h);
 492: 		check_u("[LIVE] a WRITE DOES deassert the irq", irq_deasserts, 1);
 493: 
 494: 		irq_deasserts = 0;
 495: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[0] = 2;
 496: 		memset(buf, 0xff, sizeof(buf));
 497: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_CLEAR, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
 498: 		check_u("a READ must NOT deassert the irq", irq_deasserts, 0);
 499: 
 500: 		check_u("a READ of TIMER_1_CLEAR must NOT clear pending",
 501: 		    h->pending_timer_interrupts[0], 2);
 502: 
 503: 		/*  ENABLE.  A measuring seat named this the worst of five one-token
 504: 		    escapes: widen the guard with `|| (timer_control & TIMER_ENABLE)`
 505: 		    and it sweeps the whole table, because every other row here uses
 506: 		    control = 0 or PERIODIC and never TIMER_ENABLE.
 507: 
 508: 		    It is the worst because of WHICH state it re-opens: a DISABLED
 509: 		    timer can never hold a pending interrupt, so the mutant re-opens
 510: 		    the defect on precisely the state this round exists for.  */
 511: 		h->timer_control[0] = TIMER_ENABLE;
 512: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[0] = 2;
 513: 		memset(buf, 0xff, sizeof(buf));
 514: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_CLEAR, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
 515: 		check_u("an ENABLED timer READ does not clear",
 516: 		    h->pending_timer_interrupts[0], 2);
 517: 		h->timer_control[0] = 0;
 518: 
 519: 		/*  WIDTH.  `&& len == 4` sweeps every row above -- they all use a
 520: 		    four-byte access.  The same shape as the wdc detector's own width
 521: 		    hole, found the same day in a different file.  */
 522: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[0] = 2;
 523: 		memset(buf, 0xff, sizeof(buf));
 524: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_CLEAR, buf, 2, MEM_READ, h);
 525: 		check_u("a 2-byte READ does not clear either",
 526: 		    h->pending_timer_interrupts[0], 2);
 527: 
 528: 		/*  *** THE ANCHOR ROWS, and the premise they correct is mine. ***
 529: 		    This round's record said 'every row exercises timer 1'.  It does
 530: 		    not: TIMER_1_CLEAR is 0x30C and (0x30C >> 5) & 3 == 0, so every
 531: 		    row above is an INDEX-0 row.  That is exactly why `timer_nr = 0`
 532: 		    and a widened fold mask each score a clean sweep -- the file
 533: 		    claims FOUR addresses and carried no row distinguishing any of
 534: 		    them.
 535: 
 536: 		    0x32C selects index 1 and 0x36C index 3.  Asserting the NEIGHBOUR
 537: 		    is untouched is what makes these ANCHORED rather than merely
 538: 		    distinct: a re-map that collides on one probed cell still reds.
 539: 		    */
 540: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[0] = 5;
 541: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[1] = 2;
 542: 		memset(buf, 0, sizeof(buf));
 543: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_2_CLEAR, buf, 4, MEM_WRITE, h);
 544: 		check_u("0x32C decrements timer index 1",
 545: 		    h->pending_timer_interrupts[1], 1);
 546: 		check_u("...and leaves index 0 untouched",
 547: 		    h->pending_timer_interrupts[0], 5);
 548: 
 549: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[3] = 2;
 550: 		memset(buf, 0, sizeof(buf));
 551: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_4_CLEAR, buf, 4, MEM_WRITE, h);
 552: 		check_u("0x36C decrements timer index 3",
 553: 		    h->pending_timer_interrupts[3], 1);
 554: 		check_u("...and index 0 is still untouched",
 555: 		    h->pending_timer_interrupts[0], 5);
 556: 
 557: 		/*  A READ AT A NON-ZERO INDEX, and it is the row the anchor pair
 558: 		    above could not be: those drive WRITES, so a guard widened by
 559: 		    `|| timer_nr != 0` passes them for the ordinary reason and
 560: 		    nothing reds.  MEASURED: that mutant escaped the 32-row table
 561: 		    until this row existed.
 562: 
 563: 		    The general shape is worth naming: an ANCHOR row proves WHICH
 564: 		    cell an access lands on, and a GUARD row proves the access is
 565: 		    refused.  A mutant that widens the guard per-index needs both
 566: 		    properties in ONE row, and neither existing row had them.  */
 567: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[1] = 2;
 568: 		memset(buf, 0xff, sizeof(buf));
 569: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_2_CLEAR, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
 570: 		check_u("a READ at index 1 does not clear",
 571: 		    h->pending_timer_interrupts[1], 2);
 572: 
 573: 		/*  THE COMMON TAIL, and it is the row the fix's own comment needed.
 574: 		    That comment says the `break` is deliberate because an early
 575: 		    `return` would skip

 577: 		                memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len, odata);
 578: 		    and hand a guest an untouched buffer.  Nothing tested that: buf was
 579: 		    memset to 0xff and never read, so `break` -> `return 1` passed both
 580: 		    rows.  A stated rationale with no row behind it is a claim, not a
 581: 		    property.  Same access as the row above -- the question is what that
 582: 		    call LEFT IN THE BUFFER, not what it did to the device.
 583: 
 584: 		    Zero is the tail writing an untouched odata, which is also what the
 585: 		    pre-fix code returned; the datasheet specifies no read value for a

 587: 		back = memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4);
 588: 		check_u("a READ still runs the common tail", back, 0);
 589: 
 590: 		/*  PERIODIC READ.  The rows above use control = 0, so the periodic arm
 591: 		    of the guarded body never runs and a guard widened to admit a

 595: 
 596: 		    This is the clause-(d) row for this fix: it keeps the guard and
 597: 		    nulls its repair on the one path the other rows cannot reach.  */
 598: 		h->timer_control[0] = TIMER_MODE_PERIODIC;
 599: 		h->timer_load[0]    = 0x1234;
 600: 		h->timer_value[0]   = 0x9999;	/*  deliberately != load  */
 601: 		h->pending_timer_interrupts[0] = 2;
 602: 		memset(buf, 0xff, sizeof(buf));
 603: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_CLEAR, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
 604: 		check_u("a PERIODIC read must not reload the counter",
 605: 		    h->timer_value[0], 0x9999);
 606: 		check_u("a PERIODIC read must not clear pending either",
 607: 		    h->pending_timer_interrupts[0], 2);
 608: 
 609: 		free(h);

A one-statement mutation appears able to pass all 707 rows through that gap. I’m verifying the call sequences and finishing the arithmetic and disposition checks.

def trace(calls,mut=None):
    lat=[0]*5; alarms=[]
    for i in calls:
        alarm=not lat[i]
        if alarm:
            lat[i]=1
            if mut and i==mut[0]: lat[mut[1]]=0
        alarms.append(int(alarm))
    return alarms,lat

 798: 	 *  ever creates a struct timer, so no callback can fire before a bound
 799: 	 *  exists.  Initialised here anyway, because that is a two-step argument
 800: 	 *  about a signal handler and a later edit adding a timer elsewhere would

 803: 	 *  *** THE SEED IS 1, NOT THE CEILING, AND THE FIRST VERSION HAD THIS
 804: 	 *  BACKWARDS. ***  It called the ceiling "the safe default: it can never
 805: 	 *  throttle, only bound."  The ceiling is 68,157,440 -- at cats' 762.94 Hz
 806: 	 *  that is TWENTY-FIVE HOURS of un-acked backlog, i.e. pre-fix behaviour
