codex
VERDICT: DO NOT SHIP -- X11 does not detect re-arming an already latched site, so the claimed class closure does not hold.

1. **Q1 — Enumeration and observable coverage**

**(a)** The contract names the relevant inputs and body-tested partitions: direction, own latch clear/set, and zero/nonzero `irq_status & new_enable` after a legal Clear write. The failure is chiefly in observing the resulting state transitions.

**(b)** The families provide these checks:

| Family | What it actually holds |
|---|---|
| X6 | Decline return code, first/repeat announcement, four register fields, interrupt calls, and declined-read data. These channels distinguish the stated direct mutations. |
| X7 | Announcements following legal accesses. Useful for detecting latch re-arming; it cannot establish legal values or effects. Enable writes use len 4 only. |
| X9 | Legal values/effects, interrupt calls, announcements, and subsequent latch availability, with latches initially clear. Enable writes use len 4 only. |
| X10 | Legal read values across widths with all latches set; Clear-write effects only for len 4/data `0x70`. |
| X11 | The second site’s announcement, four register fields, and interrupt calls. **It never verifies either latch afterward**, nor the declined-read result when the second site is Clear. |
| X12 | Selected Clear-write values/effects across widths, with latches initially clear. |

Consequently, the contract’s “legal × width × state … every latch set” exceeds the actual Clear-write coverage.

**(c)** F7’s two central premises hold. No decline body reads `idata`. Enumerating the fold:

```text
0x180 & ~0x060 = 0x180
0x184 & ~0x060 = 0x184
0x18c & ~0x060 = 0x18c
0x280 & ~0x060 = 0x280
0x284 & ~0x060 = 0x284
```

Enumerating every address in the original `0x300..0x36c` range also produces no mapping onto those five sites.

Arbitrary exact-value predicates, added address aliases, unrelated-register semantics, wide declined-write data, and dependencies on unrelated fields can remain disclosed exclusions under the stated bound. But exclusions for joint conditions cannot remove the native post-Clear overlap test or latch preservation within an expressly covered X11 pair. Those are obligations inside the claimed space.

2. **Q2 — An undisclosed escape**

Add one statement inside IRQ_STATUS’s first-decline body, immediately after its own latch assignment at [the frozen handler](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/_scratchpad/fbd5/q2c/frozen/dev_footbridge.c:577):

```c
d->warned_irq_raw_w = 0;
```

**Prediction: all 707 rows remain green.**

The decisive surviving row is:

```text
X11 first write IRQ_STATUS after write IRQ_RAW_STATUS latched: announced once
```

It latches RAW, then declines STATUS. The mutation clears RAW’s latch while STATUS announces exactly once. Every [X11 assertion](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/_scratchpad/fbd5/q2c/frozen/diff_footbridge.c:1150) still passes. The instance is then freed.

A subsequent RAW decline would announce again. That is an explicitly prohibited latch re-arm, involving exactly the ordered pair X11 claims to cover—not an arbitrary field value, address alias, or combination beyond its pairs.

I checked this through an in-memory latch projection of the frozen detector’s 464 instance sequences and 2,587 declined calls. This mutation changes **zero announcement traces** and leaves a different final latch vector only in that X11 pair. Across all 20 ordered cross-site zero-store insertions, **10 escape**: the `4+3+2+1` choices where the reset target follows the executing site in the usual five-site order. This is static/model verification, **not a compiled-mutant measurement**.

There is also an undisclosed width/latch combination: adding `&& (len == 4 || !d->warned_irq_clear_r)` to Clear’s write-direction predicate would silently discard narrow legal writes after latching. X12 uses clear latches; X7/X10 use word writes.

The repair should check the complete latch vector and repeat both sites in every X11 pair, including returned data for Clear reads. Clear-write coverage should cross the claimed widths and data representatives with both latch states. Measuring these mutations against the standalone detector would validate the repair.

3. **Q3 — Dispositions**

| Item | Disposition | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| F1 | **UPHOLD** | X10 observes the previously missing value channel for its specified accesses. |
| F2 | **OVERTURN** | X11 closes the cited guard mutations but does not establish latch preservation across the pair. |
| F3 | **REFINE** | Narrow writes are covered with clear latches; narrow writes after latching remain absent. |
| F4 | **REFINE** | Wide-data additions are useful, but the previous data-domain premise is false, and wide writes with set latches remain absent. |
| F5 | **UPHOLD** | Zero/ONES supplies both truth values for each of the 32 individual bits in each field; this does not prove arbitrary combinations. |
| F6 | **UPHOLD** | ONES makes direct byte-lane truncations observable. |
| F7 | **UPHOLD** | The decline/data independence and fold invariance arguments check out. |
| F8 | **REFINE** | Excluding arbitrary exact constants is defensible; “no finite set” is mathematically false. Explicitly include exact `idata` values in the disclosure. |
| F9 | **UPHOLD** | Keeping the names is defensible: the comparison checks the entire returned word, including expected zero bits. |
| F10 | **UPHOLD** | The revised X9 header names its actual widths and write set. |
| F11 | **UPHOLD** | The specifically listed corrections are present. |
| F12 | **UPHOLD** | Counts do not establish identities; a separate detector-integrity follow-up is reasonable. |
| F13 | **REFINE** | Separate repair is reasonable, but “two arms #545 did not edit” is inaccurate: Clear is an edited arm; its legal branch was unchanged. |
| F14 | **UPHOLD** | Additional guest reset reads are not required by the settled offline floor. |
| D1 | **UPHOLD** | Concrete in-scope escapes justified rejection; the mere existence of 153 greens would not, since some are equivalent or excluded. |

**The accepted disposition that is wrong as a completed closure is F2.** F3/F4 also need narrower claims or additional combinations.

F4’s factual correction matters: [P8 already writes `0x10`](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/_scratchpad/fbd5/q2c/frozen/diff_footbridge.491.c:694). The actual decoded legal Clear data sets are:

```text
491 rows: {0, 0x10, 0x70}
707 rows: {0, 0x10, 0x70, 0xff, 0xffff, 0xffffffff}
```

The narrow “all-ones” producers emit `0xff` and `0xffff`, not `0xffffffff`.

4. **Q4 — Records and arithmetic**

The counts are correct. Independently enumerating the loops and archived FIX output gives:

```text
Base: 32 timer rows + 1 identity = 33
P0–P9: 5+13+13+14+13+13+1+2+2+2 = 78
X1–X5: 12+11+3+10+2 = 38
X6:  (4 write sites × 2 data + 1 read site) × 3 widths × 4 states × 2 occurrences = 216
X7:  (8 writes + 9 reads × 3 widths) × 4 states = 140
X8:  5 value checks + 1 announcement check = 6
X9:  (5 reads × 3 widths + 8 writes) × 4 states = 92
X10: (5 reads × 3 widths + 1 write) × 4 states = 64
X11: 5 × 4 ordered distinct pairs = 20
X12: 5 write configurations × 4 states = 20

707 = 33+78+38+216+140+6+92+64+20+20
X[1–8] = 38+216+140+6 = 400
```

The historical increments also balance:

```text
333 = 33+78+222
491 = 333+54+35+69
707 = 491+54+35+23+64+20+20
```

Applying the gate’s selectors to the archived FIX log yields exactly its pins: **78, 400, 216, 140, 6, 92, 64, 20, 20**, with 707 unique row names overall. The rescore totals enumerate to **434/153/57**; the carried report contains **59 entries, zero reported lost kills**.

These statements need correction:

- The contract overstates legal-width coverage with latches set.
- The X10–X12 history says every previous legal write carried `0` or `0x70`; P8 carried `0x10`.
- [X8’s header](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/_scratchpad/fbd5/q2c/frozen/diff_footbridge.c:955) attributes ONES coverage to “X9-X12,” but X11 always uses `YNEW(0)`, the seeded state.
- “No finite set of states holds them all” is false for fixed-width fields. Each `uint32_t` has 4,294,967,296 values. Say that exhaustive enumeration is impractical and outside the contract.
- “The bodies test no values” should say they do not compare fields against arbitrary constants; Clear expressly tests whether the resulting overlap is zero.

5. **Q5 — Other dispositions**

Yes. **FILE is a tracking action**, compatible with fixing now, fixing in a separate change, deferring with an explicit acceptance criterion, or accepting the residual limitation.

For F12, an expected-name manifest could be a separate small gate change. For F13, a regression using the real interrupt callbacks could establish the defect before a separate product repair. Neither requires expanding this closing pass; neither excuses the X11 gap.

VERDICT: DO NOT SHIP -- X11 does not detect re-arming an already latched site, so the claimed class closure does not hold.

1. **Q1 — Enumeration and observable coverage**

**(a)** The contract names the relevant inputs and body-tested partitions: direction, own latch clear/set, and zero/nonzero `irq_status & new_enable` after a legal Clear write. The failure is chiefly in observing the resulting state transitions.

**(b)** The families provide these checks:

| Family | What it actually holds |
|---|---|
| X6 | Decline return code, first/repeat announcement, four register fields, interrupt calls, and declined-read data. These channels distinguish the stated direct mutations. |
| X7 | Announcements following legal accesses. Useful for detecting latch re-arming; it cannot establish legal values or effects. Enable writes use len 4 only. |
| X9 | Legal values/effects, interrupt calls, announcements, and subsequent latch availability, with latches initially clear. Enable writes use len 4 only. |
| X10 | Legal read values across widths with all latches set; Clear-write effects only for len 4/data `0x70`. |
| X11 | The second site’s announcement, four register fields, and interrupt calls. **It never verifies either latch afterward**, nor the declined-read result when the second site is Clear. |
| X12 | Selected Clear-write values/effects across widths, with latches initially clear. |

Consequently, the contract’s “legal × width × state … every latch set” exceeds the actual Clear-write coverage.

**(c)** F7’s two central premises hold. No decline body reads `idata`. Enumerating the fold:

```text
0x180 & ~0x060 = 0x180
0x184 & ~0x060 = 0x184
0x18c & ~0x060 = 0x18c
0x280 & ~0x060 = 0x280
0x284 & ~0x060 = 0x284
```

Enumerating every address in the original `0x300..0x36c` range also produces no mapping onto those five sites.

Arbitrary exact-value predicates, added address aliases, unrelated-register semantics, wide declined-write data, and dependencies on unrelated fields can remain disclosed exclusions under the stated bound. But exclusions for joint conditions cannot remove the native post-Clear overlap test or latch preservation within an expressly covered X11 pair. Those are obligations inside the claimed space.

2. **Q2 — An undisclosed escape**

Add one statement inside IRQ_STATUS’s first-decline body, immediately after its own latch assignment at [the frozen handler](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/_scratchpad/fbd5/q2c/frozen/dev_footbridge.c:577):

```c
d->warned_irq_raw_w = 0;
```

**Prediction: all 707 rows remain green.**

The decisive surviving row is:

```text
X11 first write IRQ_STATUS after write IRQ_RAW_STATUS latched: announced once
```

It latches RAW, then declines STATUS. The mutation clears RAW’s latch while STATUS announces exactly once. Every [X11 assertion](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/_scratchpad/fbd5/q2c/frozen/diff_footbridge.c:1150) still passes. The instance is then freed.

A subsequent RAW decline would announce again. That is an explicitly prohibited latch re-arm, involving exactly the ordered pair X11 claims to cover—not an arbitrary field value, address alias, or combination beyond its pairs.

I checked this through an in-memory latch projection of the frozen detector’s 464 instance sequences and 2,587 declined calls. This mutation changes **zero announcement traces** and leaves a different final latch vector only in that X11 pair. Across all 20 ordered cross-site zero-store insertions, **10 escape**: the `4+3+2+1` choices where the reset target follows the executing site in the usual five-site order. This is static/model verification, **not a compiled-mutant measurement**.

There is also an undisclosed width/latch combination: adding `&& (len == 4 || !d->warned_irq_clear_r)` to Clear’s write-direction predicate would silently discard narrow legal writes after latching. X12 uses clear latches; X7/X10 use word writes.

The repair should check the complete latch vector and repeat both sites in every X11 pair, including returned data for Clear reads. Clear-write coverage should cross the claimed widths and data representatives with both latch states. Measuring these mutations against the standalone detector would validate the repair.

3. **Q3 — Dispositions**

| Item | Disposition | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| F1 | **UPHOLD** | X10 observes the previously missing value channel for its specified accesses. |
| F2 | **OVERTURN** | X11 closes the cited guard mutations but does not establish latch preservation across the pair. |
| F3 | **REFINE** | Narrow writes are covered with clear latches; narrow writes after latching remain absent. |
| F4 | **REFINE** | Wide-data additions are useful, but the previous data-domain premise is false, and wide writes with set latches remain absent. |
| F5 | **UPHOLD** | Zero/ONES supplies both truth values for each of the 32 individual bits in each field; this does not prove arbitrary combinations. |
| F6 | **UPHOLD** | ONES makes direct byte-lane truncations observable. |
| F7 | **UPHOLD** | The decline/data independence and fold invariance arguments check out. |
| F8 | **REFINE** | Excluding arbitrary exact constants is defensible; “no finite set” is mathematically false. Explicitly include exact `idata` values in the disclosure. |
| F9 | **UPHOLD** | Keeping the names is defensible: the comparison checks the entire returned word, including expected zero bits. |
| F10 | **UPHOLD** | The revised X9 header names its actual widths and write set. |
| F11 | **UPHOLD** | The specifically listed corrections are present. |
| F12 | **UPHOLD** | Counts do not establish identities; a separate detector-integrity follow-up is reasonable. |
| F13 | **REFINE** | Separate repair is reasonable, but “two arms #545 did not edit” is inaccurate: Clear is an edited arm; its legal branch was unchanged. |
| F14 | **UPHOLD** | Additional guest reset reads are not required by the settled offline floor. |
| D1 | **UPHOLD** | Concrete in-scope escapes justified rejection; the mere existence of 153 greens would not, since some are equivalent or excluded. |

**The accepted disposition that is wrong as a completed closure is F2.** F3/F4 also need narrower claims or additional combinations.

F4’s factual correction matters: [P8 already writes `0x10`](C:/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/_scratchpad/fbd5/q2c/frozen/diff_footbridge.491.c:694). The actual decoded legal Clear data sets are:

```text
491 rows: {0, 0x10, 0x70}
707 rows: {0, 0x10, 0x70, 0xff, 0xffff, 0xffffffff}
```

The narrow “all-ones” producers emit `0xff` and `0xffff`, not `0xffffffff`.

X7:  (8 writes + 9 reads × 3 widths) × 4 states = 140
X8:  5 value checks + 1 announcement check = 6
X9:  (5 reads × 3 widths + 8 writes) × 4 states = 92
X10: (5 reads × 3 widths + 1 write) × 4 states = 64
X11: 5 × 4 ordered distinct pairs = 20
X12: 5 write configurations × 4 states = 20

707 = 33+78+38+216+140+6+92+64+20+20
X[1–8] = 38+216+140+6 = 400
```
